STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> TITLE: Anosognosia [from NecroLepsy]
>  ARTIST: NecroPolo
> COMMENT: GMC2.0 "It has a twisty story. The original song was created in
>          2012 when I started to create prototype mixing sessions that
>          became the base of my albums AENTHERA (2016), HYPERGAME (2017)
>          and DESTRONNAUTH (2019). Virtually this one was the very first
>          demo track that I made for the upcoming chip-metal albums,
>          other tracks were just finished first. Finally it had its place
>          on the 4th album called NECROLEPSY (2022). At the moment of
>          releasing this SID in 2021 the album hasn't been even mixed down
>          yet so the SID cover version created for Vincenzo's C64 Grand
>          Tour Challenge became the first public release." (NP)

> COMMENT: MS2.1 "Tune has a long way home: initially it existed as an audio

>          I created six or seven of SID worktunes and some short jingles. The
>          game did not reach the finish line so most of tunes eat dust. For
>          Martin's MS2 online compo I fed the music data of the "Coastal"
>          subtune into the proggie. Checking the tune, Martin asked if he
>          can use it for the loading screen of Berzerk Redux cart version.
>          It is one of my fav new-old games so I was really happy to provide
>          that." (NP)

> COMMENT: FC4.0 "During early May of 2011 a pack of worn disks were returned
>          to me that included some old SIDs and other workfiles in a massively
>          unorganised form. Back in time, many times I just saved workfiles
>          to gamedisks spare space and I used messy filenames. I had
>          a phone/note book to keep some order in the mess. 20 years later
>          this book became my only memory of certain songs. According to it,
>          I still have 40-50 disks lurking around somewhere. This short tune
>          was hiding on one of the retrieved old disks and as archives say
>          it's one of my earliest SIDs." (NP)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> COMMENT: SW1.9-EMU "The Shadow introduced me to Zarko who was working on a C64
>          game project called Bricks'n'Walls, under Avatar flag. Zarko is a
>          very cool and humbe guy with an awesome work ethic so we quickly
>          got along well. His game project was a tribute to wallbreaker games
>          like Krakout or Arkanoid. Out of respect, we agreed on adding a couple
>          of tune covers of legendary wallbreaker music to the game project. It
>          includes a lot of subtunes. The ingame ones were tricky as they had to
>          be 2-channel as the 3rd channel was reserved for sound FX. The audio
>          engine that provided all (+SFX) was SID-Wizard 1.9 tracker by Hermit
>          who also offered some very good tech assistance on the way. About
>          composing, all of the songs were recorded with a MIDI keyboard,
>          arranged inside Reaper DAW and exported as 3-channel MIDI. The
>          SWMconvert.exe turned it into 3-channel note data for SID-Wizard where
>          the sounds became added / tweaked and the data assigned to subtunes.
>          Using a MIDI converter is a very fast process. This way, the whole
>          project took about the same time as feeding music data to a tracker
>          manually for a single tune would." (NP)

>          black-screen releases that ended up to be a scroll + pic demo,
>          almost entirely done by me. Hermit helped me out in coding, fixing
>          bugs. The tune itself is re-assembled from different pre-1994
>          parts. The intro engine sound is not digi. I created it in 1992 as a
>          tribute to Nigel Mansell who won the F1 championship that year." (NP)

>          retro style PC racing game called Project Downforce around 2011.
>          The original sounded like an old chiptune from some SNES machine
>          so SID sound is not too far from its original form, just longer.
>          A couple of years later Jazzcat asked a rocking SID for a dismag
>          and I expanded the original tune, using Hermit's SWMconvert tool
>          that he made for SIDwizard. The title refers to legendary PANTERA
>          guitarist Dimebag Darrell. All the riffs and solos reflect his
>          style, I guess he would make something like that if he made C64
>          music. This SID was finally not released for the diskmag but
>          in 2014 @ÁROK Party SID compo, placed #3." (NP)

> COMMENT: GT2.75 "During Vincenzo's C64GTC I realised how magnificent
>          centerpiece GoatTracker2 is. It sounds good and also so many
>          trackers can load and read its workfiles. So, if you learn
>          of how to use GoatTracker2, you can inject the data into
>          SID-Wizard, NinjaTracker or SID-Factory so that way
>          you can use various music driver codes. It is handy when you
>          make something for a raster-hungry demo and convert the tune to
>          NinjaTracker. About the title: my car visited the mechanic
>          shop during the time and the guy there said things like
>          crankshaft pulley, rollbar, flywheel or exhaust manifold." (NP)

>          amazes me. This sid cover needed roughly one afternoon from idea
>          to finish. No repetitions, all parts are supposed to be different.
>          Also a new solo was added (not in the original) that was actually
>          played and recorded live on a keyboard just like I do on an album.
>          The MIDI converter gives quite some freedom for instrumentalists I
>          guess. Programming manually melody content of this depth would be
>          quite a mindfuck. For me it took days before SID-Wizard. In some
>          cases, a week perhaps." (NP)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> COMMENT: SW1.9-EMU "I think I don't have to introduce Dirk Gently's
>          Holistic Detective Agency by the amazing Douglas Adams. But as it
>          happened, it was his other work The Hitchhiker's Guide to the
>          Galaxy that I read around the time. I had to wait a lot during a
>          series of medical examinations and I thought it is a good excuse
>          to re-read the trilogy of five, with all the spinoffs. So instead
>          of the actual title, it is rather a sort of tribute to the great
>          Douglas Adams, my most fav writer. About the usual tech side,
>          as a SID-Wizard Emulator tune, apart from setting the sounds
>          in the tracker, 99% of the work was done with a MIDI keyboard
>          such as composing, orchestrating and performing / recording.
>          Of course I used Hermit's smart MIDI converter tool
>          SWMconver.exe as always." (NP)
>          Won first place at the Arok 2024 music competition.

>
> TITLE: The Raid [from the Amiga game Defender of the Crown] (0:00)
>  ARTIST: Jim Cuomo
>   TITLE: The Saxon Maid [from the Amiga game Defender of the Crown] (1:47)
>  ARTIST: Jim Cuomo
> COMMENT: NT2.03 "Shortly after my SID remix album 'rakBIT' was released
>          including a remix of Richard's brilliant 'Rimrunner', Jazzcat
>          contacted me and asked to join the Richard Joseph (R.I.P.)
>          tribute demo project with a C64 remake of one of Richard's great
>          SIDs, a tune of my choice. It's been an honour to be invited: I
>          respect Richard and admire his works much, not to mention the fine
>          array of SID composers who also contributed to the demo. This remake
>          was made in a rather short time and it includes very old ideas of
>          mine that are rooted back in '89. The SID was named after the guitar
>          that I used to grab the notes." (NP)
>          Covers /MUSICIANS/J/Joseph_Richard/Defender_of_the_Crown.sid,
>          Tunes #7 and #10.

>
> COMMENT: GMC1.6 + SW1.9-EMU "This game music pack has approximately a
>          decade-old history. When we started to create stuff with LHS
>          he mentioned that he has a race game project that was already 10
>          years old even by then. The very first music track I created for
>          the game was a special edition of my engine FX tune Cadmium,
>          around 2010. Then years passed, SID-Wizard and MIDI conversion
>          came out. Many tiny tunes became added and detached from the
>          project, like yellowstorm.sid that was intended to be used in
>          GP Cars initially. Then the game started to get its final form
>          in 2022, so did the music. The in-game tunes were created with
>          the super-flexible SW-EMU. I must emphasize that the amazing
>          3...2...1... FX and most others are not mine. They were
>          created by Hermit who is a much better sound designer than
>          I've ever been." (NP)

> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> COMMENT: GMC1.6 "My second (and last) game tune back in the time, created for
>          an odd software developer called zOnE. Geister was a minimalistic
>          and badass turn-based sci-fi strategy, a bit similar to Laser Squad.
>          All the source code was destroyed when zOnE's place burnt to dust in
>          October, 1994. zOnE stopped writing games and I stopped writing C64
>          music afterwards. Some riffs of Geister survived in my later band
>          projects. This collection was onefiled by iAN CooG, packing together
>          independent workfiles." (NP)
>          All tunes are made in 1994, released in 2009.

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> COMMENT: SW1.4 + SW1.7 "This is the sibling of the other gameproject that I
>          made for AVATAR because it had the very same workflow. I turned on
>          my synth and started playing. I created a 3-channel session file
>          in Reaper studio software with all the subtunes included then I
>          converted the whole pack with Hermit's SWMconvert.exe, created
>          instruments, adjusted sounds, packed exported files and so on.
>          I wanted to use SIDwizard 1.7 because of its nice advanced
>          functions but it turned out that it supports 6 subtunes only and I
>          needed a couple more so I used the older SW1.4 for the ingame tunes
>          instead because it can handle 16 subs. Only the title screen uses
>          SW1.7. Despite the big number of subtunes, it was a really fast and
>          fluent project." (NP)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> COMMENT: SW1.4 "It was one of the most challenging tasks I've ever done on
>          many sides. Partly because I had to pack 16 subtunes in $15FF space
>          and partly because I had extremely little time for the project
>          because of album recording project I was working on at the time.
>          So I created all the subtunes with a zero data separator part with
>          a MIDI keyboard and DAW software Reaper as 3-channel MIDI, fed it
>          with SWMconvert.exe and set the sounds. Instead of days the whole
>          project was ready in an afternoon's time, all in all. I had to keep
>          in mind from recordnig the first MIDI note that only a couple of
>          blocks are allowed per subtune because of the memory limit. Due to
>          planning, everything worked fine and it became the most efficient
>          SID project I've done to date." (NP)
> (#1)

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> TITLE: Végtelen vidék [from album Sárkányfejezet]
>  ARTIST: Diabolus in Musica
> COMMENT: SW1.8 "That is where 20 minutes can bring you with SID-Wizard.
>          I wanted to do something for the final round of C64 Grand Tour
>          challenge that no other tracker can do so I recorded a short
>          tune with an Arturia Keylab49 keyboard in Reaper, quantised
>          and arranged channels for a better conversion, exported a
>          3-channel MIDI file, ran swmconvert.exe on it and set the
>          sounds and some other stuff. The music data is not touched so
>          virtually it is me playing the keyboard. SID-Wizard is a
>          beast of workflow. The title refers to Yennefer from Witcher
>          books, also my childhood as our garden was full of lilac and
>          gooseberries. By today, due to climate change they all gone
>          around my area so that fragrance and memories of the taste
>          also remind me of good people who gone and it completely rips
>          my heart out. After those flashbacks I can't find my way for
>          days. Anyway, this tune is included on Diabouls in Musica LP
>          with lyrics and acoustic guitars. Coupled with deep lyrics
>          I always have to focus to the extreme to not to collapse mentally
>          during live shows when we perform this song called 'Végtelen
>          vidék' or 'Infinite Landscape' as it is about letting it go and
>          saying goodbye forever inevitably." (NP)

>          About the creation itself it is basically me playing a MIDI
>          keyboard much slower then the MIDI data is injected directly
>          into SID-Wizard using the SWMconvert.exe tool." (NP)

> COMMENT: SW1.9-EMU "The the multplatform version of SID-Wizard 1.9 was
>          quite a big thing for me. Before the release I did a lot of
>          testing for Hermit and the result was this tune. I am so honored
>          to be involved in the test team. SW-EMU as we called it, is a
>          beast, the king of workflow. I've been using that since then,
>          almost excusively. About the tune itself, around the covid
>          lockdowns I started to enjoy making these tunes with cleaner
>          orchestration. It is basically a bass synth, a melody line and
>          a drum sequencer. I record these on a synth then convert into
>          a C64 tune with SWMconvert.exe. It adds a little personality
>          to the playing as I play the parts a little different from
>          beat to beat I guess. 3 channels and that's it. Maybe it doesn't
>          sound as heavy as my older stuff where the SID8580 chip wanted
>          to fall apart but still dark as hell, just a different way." (NP)

>          tracker and tune was ready to deliver. Easy-peasy." (NP)

| 1 | 2 | pcm-difference-with-similar-write-sequence | 1.710e-2; 1755 | ΔRMS 2.858e-3; writes 1755; prefix 1755; drift 13; derived | identical PCM; writes 1755; prefix 1755; drift 0; derived | ΔRMS 2.858e-3; writes 1755; prefix 1755; drift 13 |

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> COMMENT: SW1.9-EMU "To create this demo we used a video sequencer and
>          bits'n'pieces from my music score for TV drama 'Henry IV' by
>          Pirandello. Finally it never became released so Time Paradox is the
>          first appearance. This demo is very special to me because we met Level
>          64 guys the very first time on X party '26 where it was released,
>          after all those years of working together. As for the tech side it is
>          a SW tune of mine so it is a MIDI conversion. We used a video
>          sequencer to sync it all together so basically it is me playing the
>          keyboard while watching the screen action, the very same method that I
>          use for film score composing." (NP)

> COMMENT: Sosperec "Many old editors are like shitbox cars like Trabant 601 from

>          falling apart quickly. It is unfair to say Sosperec is an utter
>          shitbox as it has some clever ideas but it freezes like hell and there
>          was no documentation so... Baaaaaaaah I will never touch that again.
>          Definitely it was the hardest nut to crack during C64 Grand Tour
>          Challenge.
>          The result is this super-uninspired tune that consists of the
>          first 3 instruments that did not freeze the tracker." (NP)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> TITLE: Honolulu (from album Highway $FFFF)
>  ARTIST: NecroPolo
> COMMENT: MS2 "Basically it is an extended version of my tune called 'Thanx iAN'
>          from 2010. It was a FC4.0 tune frag from '91 and was finished two
>          decades later to iAN CooG's suggestion. It became released with Music
>          Studio 2 as an example tune.
>          Then it was ported to my friend Karl Burnett's retro game project
>          called Freeway'86 that did not reach the finish line. Still, years
>          later an album was created out of all the Nintendo64-stlye tracks.
>          This one was called Honolulu. A decade later the
>          original 'Thanx iAN' C64 tune was chosen by Martin Piper for his
>          playable parallax scroll tech demo Tusari 2. I told him like, 'hey what
>          about including an extended version that once belonged to an unreleased
>          game?'. He agreed and that's how this tune came to be in 2025." (NP)
>          See also /MUSICIANS/N/NECROPOLO/Thanx_iAN.sid

STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/N/NecroPolo/
> COMMENT: NecroPolo's own comments are denoted (NP).
>
> TITLE: Ecce Homo [from Sárkányfejezet]
>  ARTIST: Diabolus in Musica
> COMMENT: GT2.7 "This is an older worktune that I composed on guitar around
>          2000 or so. It did not fit any of my bands so it rested in the box.
>          My 'entry composition' for Avatar was a game tunes and FX pack. The
>          ingame tune needed to be slow-flow and relaxed so I dusted off this
>          one. Some time later I re-created it with Goat Tracker2 and expanded
>          it to 3-channels (the gametune is 2-channels) and added a second
>          part including heavy riffs. It wanted to release it at
>          Revision 2012 but I missed the deadline as we arrived right after
>          the oldschool music compo. So, it became released a little later at
>          Flashback 2012. Then, a massive leap to 2022 where I showed music
>          pieces for the members of my off-scene project Diabolus in musica.
>          Finally, this tune ended up to be a supermassive final track for
>          the band's 2023 album "Sárkányfejezet", titled 'Ecce Homo'. (NP)

>          intro tunes that I gave to a guy called Aero to use in his stuff
>          during the early '90s. I don't know if he finished anything afterwards.
>          I could not find him in CSDb archives so I guess not." (NP)
